\frac{V}{(\operatorname{id}-\varphi)(V)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{V})

(4.2)

mapping a representative
v\in V
to the class of the extension of
\mathbbm{1}
by
\underline{V}
whose underlying module is
V\oplus E
and whose Frobenius action is given by
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\varphi&v\cdot\mathbf{1}\\
0&\mathbf{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. Katz’s equivalence leads to a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\frac{V_{\text{good}}}{(\operatorname{id}_{V}-\varphi)(V_{\text{good}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}^{1}(\pi(\operatorname{Spec}\pazocal{O}_{E}),(T\underline{V})^{I_{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\frac{V}{(\operatorname{id}_{V}-\varphi)(V)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}^{1}(G_{E},T\underline{V})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where, by diagram chasing, the bottom row is given as follows: for
v\in V
, let
w\in V\otimes_{E}E^{s}
be such that
v=w-\varphi(\tau^{*}w)
, then
c_{v}:\rho\mapsto w-\,^{\rho}w
defines a cocycle
c_{v}:G_{E}\to T\underline{V}
whose class does not depend on the choice of
w
. The bottom row maps
v
to
c_{v}
. Hence,

(
i
)

holds if and only if
c_{x}
comes from a cocycle in
\operatorname{H}^{1}(\pi(\operatorname{Spec}\pazocal{O}_{E}),(T\underline{V})^{I_{E}})
, that is, if and only if

(

⁢
i
i

)

holds.

It remains to prove that

(

⁢
i
i

)

and

(

⁢
i
i
i

)

are equivalent. The map
v\mapsto\varphi(\sigma^{*}v)
is topologically nilpotent on
V_{\pazocal{O}}/V_{\operatorname{good}}
, as can be deduced from Lemma
4.14
, and thus
\operatorname{id}-\varphi
acts as an
\mathbb{F}
-linear automorphism on it. Therefore, the Snake Lemma implies that the natural map from the cokernel of
\operatorname{id}-\varphi
on
V_{\operatorname{good}}
to that on
V_{\pazocal{O}}
is an isomorphism. This implies
V_{\pazocal{O}}\subset V_{\operatorname{good}}+(\operatorname{id}-\varphi)(V_{\pazocal{O}})
and yields

V_{\operatorname{good}}+(\operatorname{id}_{V}-\varphi)(V)=V_{\pazocal{O}}+(\operatorname{id}_{V}-\varphi)(V)

as desired.
∎

4.2
Models of
A
-motives over a local function field

General theory

Let
R
be a commutative
\mathbb{F}
-algebra given together with an
\mathbb{F}
-algebra morphism
\kappa:A\to R
. Let
S
be a commutative ring which contains
R
as a subring. Let
\underline{M}=(M,\tau_{M})
be an
A
-motive over
S
(with characteristic morphism
A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\kappa}}{{\to}}R\to S
).

Definition 4.19
.

We define an
R
-integral model
L
for
\underline{M}
to be a finitely generated sub-
A\otimes R
-module of
M
satisfying:

(i)

L
generates
M
over
S
(equivalently, over
A\otimes S
),

(ii)

\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}L)\subset L[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
.

We say that
L
is
maximal
if it is not strictly contained in any other
R
-integral model of
\underline{M}
.

The next proposition is inspired by
[
Gar2
, Prop. 2.2]
:

Proposition 4.20
.

If
S
is obtained from
R
by localization, an
R
-model for
\underline{M}
exists.

Proof.

Let
(m_{1},\ldots,m_{s})
be generators of
M
as an
A\otimes S
-module, and let
L_{0}
be the sub-
A\otimes R
-module of
M
generated by
(m_{1},\ldots,m_{s})
. Let
d\in R
be such that
\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}L_{0})\subset d^{-1}L_{0}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
, and set
L:=dL_{0}
. We have

\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}L)=d^{q}\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}L_{0})\subset d^{q-1}L_{0}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]=d^{q-2}L[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]\subset L[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}].

Thus
L
is an
R
-model.
∎

When it exists, we have the following claim for a maximal integral model.

Lemma 4.21
.

A maximal
R
-integral model for
\underline{M}
contains all the
R
-integral models for
\underline{M}
. In particular, if it exists it is unique.

Proof.

Given
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
two
R
-integral models, their sum
L_{1}+L_{2}
again defines an
R
-integral model. Hence, if
L_{1}
is maximal, the inclusion
L_{1}\subseteq L_{1}+L_{2}
is not strict: we deduce
L_{1}+L_{2}=L_{1}
, then
L_{2}\subset L_{1}
.
∎

As for Frobenius spaces, we have the notion of a good sublattice.

Definition 4.22
.

We define an
R
-good sublattice
L
for
\underline{M}
to be a finitely generated sub-
A\otimes R
-module of
M
such that
\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}L)[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]=L[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
. We say that
L
is maximal if it is not strictly contained in any other
R
-good sublattice of
\underline{M}
.

Following the argument given in the proof of Proposition
4.11
, we obtain:

Lemma 4.23
.

Assume that there exists a maximal
R
-integral model for
\underline{M}
. Then, a maximal
R
-good sublattice for
\underline{M}
exists and is unique.

We continue this section by recording additional properties of maximal
R
-models in the general situation. Those will eventually by useful in Subsection
4.4
for the proof of Theorem
E
(Theorem
4.62
in the text) when specializing to
R\subset S
being the inclusion of a Dedekind domain in its fraction field.

Let
\underline{M}
be an
A
-motive over
S
and suppose that it admits a maximal integral
R
-integral model denoted by
M_{R}
.

Proposition 4.24
.

Let
N
be a finitely generated sub-
A\otimes R
-module of
M
such that
\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}N)\subset N[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
. Then,
N\subset M_{R}
. In particular, any element
m\in M
such that
\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}m)=m
belongs to
M_{R}
.

Proof.

It suffices to notice that the module
L
generated by
M_{R}
and
N
over
A\otimes R
is an
R
-model for
\underline{M}
, and hence
N\subset L\subset M_{R}
.
∎

Corollary 4.25
.

We have
(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M_{R})=(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M)\cap M_{R}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
.

Proof.

The inclusion
(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M_{R})\subset(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M)\cap M_{R}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
is clear. Conversely, let
m\in M_{R}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
and let
n\in M
be such that
m=n-\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}n)
. The sub-
A\otimes R
-module
\langle M_{R},n\rangle
of
M
generated by elements of
M_{R}
together with
n
over
A\otimes R
is an
R
-model for
\underline{M}
. In particular,
\langle M_{R},n\rangle\subset M_{R}
and
n\in M_{R}
.
∎

We end this subsection with a remark on the assignment
\underline{M}\mapsto M_{R}
. For it to be well-defined assume that the inclusion
R\subset S
is such that every object in
\mathscr{M}_{S}
admits a maximal
R
-integral model (this is the case when
R\subset S
is the inclusion of a Dedekind domain into its field of fractions, as will be shown below in Proposition
4.51
).

Corollary 4.26
.

Let
f:\underline{M}\to\underline{N}
be a morphism in
\mathscr{M}_{S}
. Then
f(M_{R})\subset N_{R}
. In particular, the assignment
\underline{M}\mapsto M_{R}
is functorial.

Existence and first properties over DVR

We examine the existence of maximal models over discrete valuation rings. Let
(\pazocal{O}_{E},v_{E})
be a discrete valuation ring with maximal ideal
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}_{E}
and fraction field
E
. We fix
\varpi\in\mathfrak{p}
a uniformizer. We assume that
\kappa:A\to\pazocal{O}_{E}
is such that
\kappa^{-1}(\mathfrak{p})
admits a non zero element. In this subsection, we shall be concerned by models of
A
-motives in the situation where
R\subset S
is the inclusion
\pazocal{O}_{E}\subset E
.

Let
\underline{M}
be an
A
-motive over
E
. In order to prove the existence of a maximal integral model for
\underline{M}
, we start with a series of lemmas. Let
L
and
L^{\prime}
be two finitely generated sub-
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
-modules of
M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
and assume that
L^{\prime}
generates
M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
over
(A\otimes E)[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
.

Lemma 4.27
.

There exists an integer
k
such that
\varpi^{k}L\subset L^{\prime}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
.

Proof.

We have
L\subset M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]=\displaystyle\bigcup_{k\geq 0}\varpi^{-k}L^{\prime}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
(colimits commute). In particular, the sequence
(L\cap\varpi^{-k}L^{\prime}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}])_{k\geq 0}
defines an ascending chain of sub-
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
-modules of
L
. As
L
is Noetherian, this chain eventually stabilizes, from which we deduce
L\subset\varpi^{-k}L^{\prime}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
for
k
large enough.
∎

We denote by
[L:L^{\prime}]
the smallest integer
k
for which Lemma
4.27
holds. Before listing important properties of this numerical invariant, the following is needed:

Lemma 4.28
.

Let
T
be a flat
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
-module and let
N:=T\otimes_{\pazocal{O}_{E}}E
. Inside
N[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
,

1.

For all
a\in A
, we have
T\cap aN=aT
.

2.

We have
T=T[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]\cap N
.

Proof.

We prove
1
: if
t\in N
and
a\in A
are such that
{(a\otimes 1)t\in T}
, then
t\in T
. Indeed, the map
T/(a\otimes 1)T\to N/(a\otimes 1)N
arises as

T\otimes_{A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}}(A/aA\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}\hookrightarrow A/aA\otimes E),

and hence is injective by flatness of
T
.

We turn to
2
. Let
c
be an element of
T[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]\cap N
. Let
a\in\kappa^{-1}(\mathfrak{p})
be non zero (which exists by our assumption on
\kappa
). Then, there exists
u,v\geq 0
such that
(1\otimes\varpi^{u})c
and
(a\otimes 1-1\otimes\kappa(a))^{v}c
are in
T
. Taking
w\geq 0
such that
q^{w}\geq u,v
, we have

(a^{q^{w}}\otimes 1)c=(a\otimes 1-1\otimes\kappa(a))^{q^{w}}c+(1\otimes\kappa(a)^{q^{w}})c\in T.

By part
1
we have
(a^{q^{w}}\otimes 1)c\in T\cap a^{q^{w}}N=a^{q^{w}}T
and then
c\in T
.
∎

Lemma 4.29
.

Let
L,L^{\prime},L^{\prime\prime}
be finitely generated sub-
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
-modules of
M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
which generates it over
(A\otimes E)[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
.

(a)

If
L\subset L^{\prime}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
, then
[L:L^{\prime}]\leq 0
,

(b)

[L:L^{\prime\prime}]\leq[L:L^{\prime}]+[L^{\prime}:L^{\prime\prime}]
.

Let
T
be a finite flat sub-
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
-module of
M
which generates it over
A\otimes E
. If
L\subset M
and
L
generates
M
over
A\otimes E
, we have

(c)

[\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}L):\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}T)]=q[L:T]
.

If, in addition,
L
is a model of
\underline{M}
, then

(d)

\displaystyle[L:T]\leq\frac{[T:\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}T)]}{q-1}
.

Proof.

Property

(
a
)

is obvious. Writing
k
and
k^{\prime}
for
[L:L^{\prime}]
and
[L^{\prime}:L^{\prime\prime}]
respectively, we have
\varpi^{k}L\subset L^{\prime}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
and
\varpi^{k^{\prime}}L^{\prime}\subset L^{\prime\prime}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
and hence
\varpi^{k+k^{\prime}}L\subset L^{\prime\prime}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
proving

(
b
)

.

We prove

(
c
)

. By Lemma
4.28
,
[L:T]
is equivalently the smallest integer
k
such that
\varpi^{k}L\subset T
. In particular,
\varpi^{qk}\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}L)\subset\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}T)
and thus

[\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}L):\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}L)]\leq qk.

If this inequality was strict, we would have have
\varpi^{qk}\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}L)\subset\varpi\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}T)[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
, hence:

\displaystyle\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}(\varpi^{k}L))=\varpi^{qk}\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}L)

\displaystyle\subset\left(\varpi\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}T)[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]\right)\cap\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}M)

\displaystyle=\varpi\left(\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}T)[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]\cap\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}M)\right)

\displaystyle=\varpi\tau_{M}((\tau^{*}T)[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]\cap\tau^{*}M)

\displaystyle=\varpi\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}T)

\displaystyle\subset\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}(T\otimes_{\pazocal{O}_{E}}\varpi^{1/q}\pazocal{O}_{E^{1/q}}))

where, to pass from the first to the second line we used that
\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}M)
is an
E
-vector space, from second to third that
\tau_{M}
is injective and from third to fourth we applied Lemma
4.28
to the modules
\tau^{*}T\subset\tau^{*}M
. Above, we denoted by
E^{1/q}
the field of
q
-roots of elements of
E
and by
\pazocal{O}_{E^{1/q}}
its ring of integers. Using the flatness of
T
, we obtain:

\varpi^{k}L\subset(T\otimes_{\pazocal{O}_{E}}\varpi^{1/q}\pazocal{O}_{E^{1/q}})\cap T=\varpi T

which contradicts
[L:T]=k
.

To conclude, we prove

(
d
)

:

\displaystyle q[L:T]

\displaystyle=[\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}L):\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}T)]\quad(\text{by~\ref{item:frobenius-index}})

\displaystyle\leq[\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}L):L]+[L:\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}T)]\quad(\text{by~\ref{item:inequality-in-chain}})

\displaystyle\leq[L:\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}T)]\quad(\text{by~\ref{item:negative}})

\displaystyle\leq[L:T]+[T:\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}T)]\quad(\text{by~\ref{item:inequality-in-chain}}).

and therefore
(q-1)[L:T]\leq[T:\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}T)]
.
∎

Let
\underline{M}
be an
A
-motive over
E
of characteristic morphism
\kappa
. We are ready to prove the existence of the maximal
\pazocal{O}_{E}
-model for
\underline{M}
.

Proposition 4.30
.

A maximal
\pazocal{O}_{E}
-model for
\underline{M}
exists and is unique. In particular, a maximal
\pazocal{O}_{E}
-good sublattice for
\underline{M}
exists and is unique.

Proof.

It is enough to show existence of a maximal integral model. Fix a flat and generating sub-
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
-module
5
5

5

Such a
T
exists: as
M
is finite projective over
A\otimes E
, it is a direct summand of free
A\otimes E
-module, say
(A\otimes E)^{s}
. Then one could take
T=(A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E})^{s}\cap M
.

T
of
M
. Denote by
c_{T}
the integer

c_{T}:=\left\lfloor-\frac{[T:\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}T)]}{q-1}\right\rfloor.

Let
U
be the
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
-module given by the sum over all the
\pazocal{O}_{E}
-models for
\underline{M}
. As
U
is non-empty by Proposition
4.20
, it generates
M
over
E
. We also have
\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}U)\subset U[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
. By Lemma
4.29

(
d
)

, any
\pazocal{O}_{E}
-model
L
of
\underline{M}
is a submodule of
\varpi^{c_{T}}T
and thus
U\subset\varpi^{c_{T}}T
. Since
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
is Noetherian,
U
is finitely generated. Hence
U
is a model of
\underline{M}
which is maximal by construction.
∎

Definition 4.31
.

We denote by
M_{\pazocal{O}}
the unique maximal
\pazocal{O}_{E}
-integral model of
\underline{M}
, and by
M_{\text{good}}
the maximal
\pazocal{O}_{E}
-good sublattice of
\underline{M}
.

As announced earlier, maximal
\pazocal{O}_{E}
-models are projective as we show next:

Theorem 4.32
.

Both
M_{\pazocal{O}}
and
M_{\operatorname{good}}
are projective over
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
.

This will result from the following general statement:

Proposition 4.33
.

Let
N
be a finite sub-
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
-module of a projective
A\otimes E
-module
M
which generates
M
over
E
. We claim that the following are equivalent:

(i)

For all
a\in A
, the equality
N\cap aM=aN
holds inside
M
, and

(ii)

the module
N
is projective.

Before proving the proposition, we recall a useful fact from dimension theory which generalizes the well-known statement that finite torsion-free modules over Dedekind domains are projective:

Lemma 4.34
.

Let
R
be a Noetherian regular integral domain of Krull dimension
n
. Let
M
be a finite torsion-free
R
-module. Then, the projective dimension of
M
is
<n
.

Proof.

We know that the projective dimension
\operatorname{pd}_{R}(M)
of
M
is
\leq n
. Suppose it is
n
. Then, there exists a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}
of
R
such that
\operatorname{pd}_{R_{\mathfrak{m}}}(M_{\mathfrak{m}})=n
. By the Auslander–Buchsbaum formula
[
Eis
, Thm. 19.9]
, we have:

\operatorname{depth}(M_{\mathfrak{m}})=\operatorname{depth}(R_{\mathfrak{m}})-n\leq 0

and then
\operatorname{depth}(M_{\mathfrak{m}})=0
. This means that
M_{\mathfrak{m}}
is torsion, a contradiction since
M
is not.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.33
.

That

(

⁢
i
i

)

implies

(
i
)

corresponds to Lemma
4.28
, point
1
.

We focus on the converse. It is enough to show that
N
is flat, which is equivalent to the injectivity of the map
\mu:I\otimes_{A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}}N\to IN
for all ideal
I
of
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
(
cf
.
[
Stack
, 00HD]
). As
M
is flat over
A\otimes E
, we have
IM\cong I\otimes_{A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}}M
and the injectivity of
\mu
is equivalent to that of
I\otimes_{A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}}N\to I\otimes_{A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}}M
. In that respect, it is sufficient to show the vanishing of the module:

\operatorname{Tor}_{1}^{A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}}(I,M/N).

(4.3)

We shall derive it by the following pair of lemmas:

Lemma 4.35
.

Let
I
be an ideal of
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
and let
P
be an
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
-module with no
A
-torsion. Then
\operatorname{Tor}^{A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}}_{1}(I,P)
has no
A
-torsion.

Proof.

As
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
has Krull dimension
2
and
I
is a finite torsion-free
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
-module, Lemma
4.34
applies to show that
I
has projective dimension
\leq 1
. By the dimension theorem
[
Wei
, Prop. 4.15]
, projective and Tor dimension coincide, hence
\operatorname{Tor}_{2}^{A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}}(I,-)=(0)
. On the other-hand, given
a\in A
non zero, the sequence
0\to P\xrightarrow{a}P\to P/aP\to 0
is exact. The induced Tor-sequence:

(0)=\operatorname{Tor}_{2}^{A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}}(I,P/aP)\to\operatorname{Tor}_{1}^{A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}}(I,P)\xrightarrow{r}\operatorname{Tor}_{1}^{A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}}(I,P)

implies that multiplication-by-
a
on
\operatorname{Tor}_{1}^{A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}}(I,P)
is injective;
i.e.
that it has no
a
-torsion. This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.36
.

For any
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
-module
P
, we have
K\otimes_{A}\operatorname{Tor}_{1}^{A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}}(I,P)=(0)
.

Proof.

As localization commutes with Tor, we have

K\otimes_{A}\operatorname{Tor}_{1}^{A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}}(I,P)\cong\operatorname{Tor}_{1}^{K\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}}(K\otimes_{A}I,K\otimes_{A}P).

But
K\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
is a Dedekind domain, thus
K\otimes_{A}I
is projective and the right-hand side is zero.
∎

We conclude the proof by showing that (
4.3
) is zero. Observe that

(
i
)

implies that
M/N
has no
A
-torsion. In particular Lemma
4.35
implies that (
4.3
) has no torsion. Lemma
4.36
then implies that (
4.3
) vanishes.
∎

To deduce Theorem
4.32
from Proposition
4.33
, we are left to show:

Lemma 4.37
.

Let
\mathfrak{a}\subset A
be an ideal. Then
M_{\pazocal{O}}\cap\mathfrak{a}M=\mathfrak{a}M_{\pazocal{O}}
and
M_{\operatorname{good}}\cap\mathfrak{a}M=\mathfrak{a}M_{\operatorname{good}}
.

Proof.

The proofs being similar, we solely explicit it in the case of
M_{\pazocal{O}}
. The inclusion
\supset
is clear. We assume
\mathfrak{a}\neq 0
and consider the sub-
A
-motive
(\mathfrak{a}M,\tau_{M})
of
\underline{M}
. If
T
is an
\pazocal{O}_{E}
-model for
(\mathfrak{a}M,\tau_{M})
, then
\mathfrak{a}^{-1}T
is an
\pazocal{O}_{E}
-model for
\underline{M}
and we have
\mathfrak{a}^{-1}T\subset M_{\pazocal{O}}
. This implies that
\mathfrak{a}M_{\pazocal{O}}
is the maximal
\pazocal{O}_{E}
-model of
(\mathfrak{a}M,\tau_{M})
so that
(\mathfrak{a}M)_{\pazocal{O}}=\mathfrak{a}(M_{\pazocal{O}})
. Therefore, the inclusion
M_{\pazocal{O}}\cap\mathfrak{a}M\subset\mathfrak{a}M_{\pazocal{O}}
follows from the fact that
M_{\pazocal{O}}\cap\mathfrak{a}M
is an
\pazocal{O}_{E}
-model for
(\mathfrak{a}M,\tau_{M})
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.32
.

For all
a\in A
, we have
M_{\pazocal{O}}\cap aM=aM_{\pazocal{O}}
(resp.
M_{\operatorname{good}}\cap aM=aM_{\operatorname{good}}
) by Lemma
4.37
. Since
M_{\pazocal{O}}
generates
M
over
E
(resp.
M_{\operatorname{good}}
generates the projective
A\otimes E
-module
M_{\operatorname{good}}\otimes_{\pazocal{O}_{E}}E
), we deduce from Proposition
4.33
that
M_{\pazocal{O}}
(resp.
M_{\operatorname{good}}
) is projective.
∎

Remark 4.38
.

Let
\underline{M}
and
\underline{N}
be two
A
-motives over
E
, and let
M_{\pazocal{O}}
and
N_{\pazocal{O}}
be their respective integral models. While the maximal integral model of
\underline{M}\oplus\underline{N}
is easily shown to be
{M_{\pazocal{O}}\oplus N_{\pazocal{O}}}
, it is not true in general that the maximal integral model of
\underline{M}\otimes\underline{N}
is the image of
M_{\pazocal{O}}\otimes_{A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}}N_{\pazocal{O}}
in
M\otimes_{A\otimes E}N
. To find a counter-example, we assume
q>2
and consider
\varpi\in\pazocal{O}_{E}
a uniformizer. We consider the
A
-motive
\underline{M}
over
E
where
M=A\otimes E
and where
\tau_{M}=\varpi\cdot\mathbf{1}
. The maximal integral model of
\underline{M}
is
M_{\pazocal{O}}=A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
. However,
\underline{M}^{\otimes(q-1)}
has
\varpi^{-1}M_{\pazocal{O}}^{\otimes(q-1)}
for maximal integral model.

Comparison with Frobenius spaces

As in Subsection
4.1
,
E
is the fraction field of a discrete valuation ring
(\pazocal{O}_{E},v_{E})
with maximal ideal
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}_{E}
. We assume that
\kappa:A\to\pazocal{O}_{E}
is such that
\kappa^{-1}(\mathfrak{p})
admits a non zero element. This implies in particular that
\kappa
is injective and hence that
\mathfrak{j}(A/\mathfrak{a}\otimes E)=A/\mathfrak{a}\otimes E
for all non zero ideal
\mathfrak{a}\subset A
. We fix a maximal ideal
\ell
of
A
. The assumption on
\kappa
implies
\mathfrak{j}(A/\ell^{n}\otimes E)=A/\ell^{n}\otimes E
for all positive integers
n
.

Let
\underline{M}
be an
A
-motive over
E
. We have canonical isomorphisms

\text{for~all~}n\geq 1:\quad M/\ell^{n}M\cong M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]/\ell^{n}M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}].

(4.4)

In particular,
\tau_{M}
defines an
A\otimes E
-linear morphism
\tau^{*}(M/\ell^{n}M)\to M/\ell^{n}M
through the composition

\tau^{*}(M/\ell^{n}M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau_{M}}}{{\longrightarrow}}M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]/\ell^{n}M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:modulo-m^n-j}}}{{\longrightarrow}}M/\ell^{n}M

which we still denote by
\tau_{M}
. The pair
(M/\ell^{n}M,\tau_{M})
thusly defined is a Frobenius space over
E
in the sense of Section
4.1
which we denote by
\underline{M}/\ell^{n}\underline{M}
. Let
L_{n}\subset M/\ell^{n}M
be its maximal integral model.

Remark 4.39
.

We caution the reader, that in general we cannot claim equality between
(M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{n}M)/\ell^{n}M
and
L_{n}
. Here is a counter-example.

Suppose that
A=\mathbb{F}[t]
, so that
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
is identified with
\pazocal{O}_{E}[t]
, and let
\ell=(t)
. Let
\kappa:A\to\pazocal{O}_{E}
be the
\mathbb{F}
-algebra morphism which maps
t
to
\varpi
. In this setting,
\mathfrak{j}
is the principal ideal of
\pazocal{O}_{E}[t]
generated by
(t-\varpi)
. Consider the
A
-motive
\underline{M}:=(E[t],f\cdot\mathbf{1})
over
E
where
f=\varpi^{q-1}-\varpi^{q-2}t
. We claim that the maximal integral model of
\underline{M}
is
\pazocal{O}_{E}[t]
. Clearly,
\pazocal{O}_{E}[t]
is an integral model for
\underline{M}
so that
\pazocal{O}_{E}[t]\subset M_{\pazocal{O}}
. Conversely, by
[
Qui2
, Thm. 4]
,
M_{\pazocal{O}}
is free of rank one over
\pazocal{O}_{E}[t]
. If
h
generates
M_{\pazocal{O}}
, there exists
b\in\pazocal{O}_{E}[t]
such that
fh^{(1)}=bh
. For
p\in E[t]
, let
v(p)
be the infinimum of the valuations of the coefficients of
p
. We have

v(h)\geq-\frac{v(f)}{q-1}=-\frac{q-2}{q-1}>-1

and
h\in\pazocal{O}_{E}[t]
. We get
M_{\pazocal{O}}\subset\pazocal{O}_{E}[t]
.

On the other-hand, the Frobenius space
(M/\ell M,\tau_{M})
is isomorphic to the pair
(\pazocal{O}_{E},\varpi^{q-1}\mathbf{1})
, whose maximal integral model is
\varpi^{-1}\pazocal{O}_{E}
, not
\pazocal{O}_{E}
.

If one wants to compare
M_{\pazocal{O}}
with
(L_{n})_{n\geq 1}
, then one wishes that
(M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{n}M)/\ell^{n}M
defines an integral model for
\underline{M}/\ell^{n}\underline{M}
for all
n\geq 1
. This is the case in Remark
4.39
, although it is not maximal, because the considered
A
-motive
\underline{M}
is effective. In general, this is not true
6
6

6

For instance, consider the
t
-motive
(E[t],(t-\varpi)^{-1}\mathbf{1})
over
E
, whose maximal
\pazocal{O}_{E}
-model is
\pazocal{O}_{E}[t]
, together with
\ell=(t)
.
. From now on, we assume

(C_{\ell})

The ideal
\ell\subset A
is such that
\kappa(\ell)\pazocal{O}_{E}=\pazocal{O}_{E}
.

The above assumption ensures that
\mathfrak{j}(A/\ell^{n}\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E})=A/\ell^{n}\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
(
e.g.
the proof Proposition
3.17
), and thus that
(M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{n}M)/\ell^{n}M
is an integral model for
(M/\ell^{n}M,\tau_{M})
.

Remark 4.40
.

Note that there always exists a maximal ideal
\ell
in
A
satisfying

(

C
ℓ

)

; in fact, any maximal ideal
\ell
coprime to
\kappa^{-1}(\mathfrak{p})
will do.

Even though we cannot claim always equality between
(M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{n}M)/\ell^{n}M
and
L_{n}
, the data of
L_{n}
for all
n\geq 1
is enough to recover
M_{\pazocal{O}}
as we show in the next theorem.

Theorem 4.41
.

Let
L_{n}
be the maximal integral model of the Frobenius space
\underline{M}/\ell^{n}\underline{M}
. Let
m\in M
. Then
m\in M_{\pazocal{O}}
if and only if
m+\ell^{n}M\in L_{n}
for all large enough positive integers
n
.

We start with some lemmas:

Lemma 4.42
.

The
\pazocal{O}_{E}
-module
L_{n}
is an
A/\ell^{n}\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
-module.

Proof.

For an elementary tensor
r\otimes f
in
A/\ell^{n}\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
, the
\pazocal{O}_{E}
-module
(r\otimes f)L_{n}
is stable by
\tau_{M}
. Indeed, we have
\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}(r\otimes f)L_{n})=(r\otimes f^{q})\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}L_{n})\subset(r\otimes f)L_{n}
. By maximality of
L_{n}
, we have
(r\otimes f)L_{n}\subset L_{n}
.
∎

Recall the notion of
range
from Definition
4.6
.

Lemma 4.43
.

Let
r_{n}
be the range of the
\pazocal{O}_{E}
-lattice
(M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{n}M)/\ell^{n}M
in
M/\ell^{n}M
. Then
(r_{n})_{n\geq 1}
is bounded.

Proof.

Let
e
and
f
be large enough positive integers such that
\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}M)\subset\mathfrak{j}^{-e}M
and
M\subset\mathfrak{j}^{-f}\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}M)
. For this choice of
e
, there is an exact sequence of
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
-modules

0\longrightarrow\tau^{*}M_{\pazocal{O}}\xrightarrow{\tau_{M}}\mathfrak{j}^{-e}M_{\pazocal{O}}\longrightarrow C_{e}\longrightarrow 0

(4.5)

where we denoted by
C_{e}
the cokernel of the former map. We claim that there exists an integer
k\geq 0
such that
C_{e}
is annihilated by
(1\otimes\varpi^{k})\mathfrak{j}^{f}
. Indeed, one could take
k=[M_{\pazocal{O}}:\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}M_{\pazocal{O}})]
(as in notations following Lemma
4.27
), as then:

\varpi^{k}M_{\pazocal{O}}\subset\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}M_{\pazocal{O}})[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]\cap\mathfrak{j}^{-f}\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}M)=\tau_{M}((\tau^{*}M_{\pazocal{O}})[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]\cap\mathfrak{j}^{-f}\tau^{*}M)=\mathfrak{j}^{-f}\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}M_{\pazocal{O}})

where, for the last inequality, we used Lemma
4.28
with respect to the flat submodule
\mathfrak{j}^{-f}\tau^{*}M_{\pazocal{O}}
of
\mathfrak{j}^{-f}\tau^{*}M
(where flatness follows from Theorem
4.32
).

The proof of the lemma will follow from the inequality
r_{n}\leq k
for all
n\geq 1
. To prove the latter, note that the range
r_{n}
is the smallest integer
r\geq 0
such that
\varpi^{r}
annihilates the cokernel of

\tau^{*}(M_{\pazocal{O}}/\ell^{n}M{O})\xrightarrow{\tau_{M}}M_{\pazocal{O}}/\ell^{n}M_{\pazocal{O}}

(4.6)

(we implicitly used
M_{\pazocal{O}}/\ell^{n}M_{\pazocal{O}}\cong(M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{n}M)/\ell^{n}M
, following Lemma
4.37
). Yet, using the right-exactness of
-\otimes_{A}A/\ell^{n}
applied to the sequence (
4.5
), together with the fact that
\mathfrak{j}
is invertible modulo
\ell^{n}
(by our assumption

(

C
ℓ

)

), the cokernel of (
4.6
) is
C_{e}/\ell^{n}C_{e}
. Hence, it is annihilated by
\varpi^{k}
and then
r_{n}\leq k
.
∎

For
n\geq 1
, let
\tilde{L}_{n}
be the inverse image in
M
of
L_{n}\subset M/\ell^{n}M
.

Proof of Theorem
4.41
.

First observe that the sequence of subsets
(\tilde{L}_{n}+\ell^{n}M)_{n\geq 1}
of
M
decreases when
n
grows: for
n\geq 1
, we have
\tilde{L}_{n+1}+\ell^{n+1}M\subset\tilde{L}_{n+1}+\ell^{n}M
and, because
(\tilde{L}_{n+1}+\ell^{n}M)/\ell^{n}M
defines an integral model for
(M/\ell^{n}M,\tau_{M})
, we also have

\tilde{L}_{n+1}+\ell^{n}M\subset\tilde{L}_{n}+\ell^{n}M.

Hence, the statement of the theorem is equivalent to the equality

M_{\pazocal{O}}=L,\quad\text{where}~L:=\bigcap_{n=1}^{\infty}{(\tilde{L}_{n}+\ell^{n}M)}.

By Lemma
4.42
,
L
is an
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
-module. The inclusion
M_{\pazocal{O}}\subset L
follows from the fact that, for all
n
,
(M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{n}M)/\ell^{n}M
is an integral model for
(M/\ell^{n}M,\tau_{M})
. To prove the converse inclusion, we show that
L
is an integral model for
\underline{M}
. From
M_{\pazocal{O}}\subset L
, one deduces that
L
generates
M
over
E
. Because
\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}(\tilde{L}_{n}+\ell^{n}M))\subset\tilde{L}_{n}+\ell^{n}M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
, we also have
\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}L)\subset L[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
. The theorem follows once we have proved that
L
is finitely generated.

Assume that
L
is not finitely generated. From the Noetherianity of
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
, for all
s\geq 0
, it follows that
L\not\subset\varpi^{-s}M_{\pazocal{O}}
. Equivalently, there exists an unbounded increasing sequence
(s_{n})_{n\geq 0}
of non-negative integers such that
\varpi^{s_{n}}L_{n}\not\subset(M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{n}M)/\ell^{n}M
. By Proposition
4.8
, the range of
(M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{n}M)/\ell^{n}M
is
>s_{n}(q-1)
. But this contradicts Lemma
4.43
.
∎

In the remainder of this subsection, we still assume

(

C
ℓ

)

. We record a useful corollary to Theorem
4.41
.

Corollary 4.44
.

Let
\underline{N}=(N,\tau_{N})
be an
A
-motive over
\pazocal{O}_{E}
and
\underline{N}_{E}
its base change to
E
. Then,
N=(N_{E})_{\pazocal{O}}=(N_{E})_{\operatorname{good}}
.

Proof.

For all
n\geq 1
,
L_{n}:=(N+\ell^{n}N_{E})/\ell^{n}N_{E}
is an integral model of the Frobenius space
(N_{E}/\ell^{n}N_{E},\tau_{N})
which further satisfies
\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}L_{n})=L_{n}
. Hence,
(N_{E}/\ell^{n}N_{E},\tau_{N})
has good reduction with
L_{n}
as the maximal integral model (
e.g.
Lemma
4.9
). Theorem
4.41
implies that the maximal integral model of
\underline{N}_{E}
is the
\ell
-adic (topological) closure of
N
in
N_{E}
. Yet, as
N
is a finite projective
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
-module, it is
\ell
-adically closed in
N_{E}
(one reduces–considering
N
as a direct summand in a finite free
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
-module and similarly for
N_{E}
by base change along
\pazocal{O}_{E}\to E
–to the question whether
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
is
\ell
-adically closed in
A\otimes E
, which is clear).
∎

There is a version of Theorem
4.41
for the maximal good model. The proof is along the same lines, so we do not repeat it.

Proposition 4.45
.

Let
T_{n}
be the maximal good sublattice of the Frobenius space
\underline{M}/\ell^{n}\underline{M}
. Let
m\in M
. Then
m\in M_{\operatorname{good}}
if and only if
m+\ell^{n}M\in T_{n}
for all large enough positive integers
n
.

For the next section, we shall not only be interested in how to recover
M_{\pazocal{O}}
from
L_{n}
, but also in how to recover
M_{\pazocal{O}}+(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M)
. While we do not give a complete answer, we at least show next how to recover its
\ell
-adic closure in
M
in the case
\underline{M}
effective. We continue with some finer technicalities.

Even if we do not have equality between
\tilde{L}_{n}+\ell^{n}M
and
M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{n}M
, the former is a good approximation of the latter as we show next.

Lemma 4.46
.

Let
n\geq 1
. The sequence
(\tilde{L}_{m}+\ell^{n}M)_{m\geq n}
is decreasing for the inclusion, stationary and converges to
M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{n}M
.

Proof.

Let
m\geq 1
.
(\tilde{L}_{m+1}+\ell^{m}M)/\ell^{m}M
is an
\pazocal{O}_{E}
-lattice stable by
\tau_{M}
in
M/\ell^{m}M
so that
\tilde{L}_{m+1}+\ell^{m}M\subset\tilde{L}_{m}+\ell^{m}M
. If
m\geq n
, we have
\tilde{L}_{m+1}+\ell^{n}M\subset\tilde{L}_{m}+\ell^{n}M
which shows that
(\tilde{L}_{m}+\ell^{n}M)_{m\geq n}
decreases. Similarly,
M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{n}M\subset\tilde{L}_{m}+\ell^{n}M
for all
m\geq n
. Because the set of
\pazocal{O}_{E}
-lattices
\Lambda
such that
M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{n}M\subseteq\Lambda\subseteq\tilde{L}_{n}+\ell^{n}M
is finite, the sequence
(\tilde{L}_{m}+\ell^{n}M)_{m\geq n}
is stationary. We denote by
\mathscr{L}_{n}
its limit. By Theorem
4.41
, we have

\mathscr{L}_{n}=\bigcap_{m=n}^{\infty}(\tilde{L}_{m}+\ell^{n}M)=\bigcap_{m=n}^{\infty}(\tilde{L}_{m}+\ell^{m}M)+\ell^{n}M=M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{n}M.

This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.47
.

There exists an unbounded and increasing sequence
(k_{n})_{n\geq 1}
of non-negative integers such that,
\tilde{L}_{n}+\ell^{n}M\subset M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{k_{n}}M
(typically,
k_{n}\leq n
for all
n
).

Proof.

For
m\geq 1
, let
I_{m}
be the set of non-negative integers
k
such that
\tilde{L}_{m}+\ell^{m}M\subset M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{k}M
.
I_{m}
is nonempty as it contains
0
.
I_{m}
is further bounded: otherwise we would have

\tilde{L}_{m}+\ell^{m}M\subset\bigcap_{k}(M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{k}M)=M_{\pazocal{O}},

(4.7)

where the last equality is due to the fact that
M_{\pazocal{O}}
is
\ell
-adically closed in
M
(by the same argument given in the proof of Corollary
4.44
, using that
M{O}
is finite projective by Theorem
4.32
). Yet, this is impossible as
\tilde{L}_{m}+\ell^{m}M
is an
A\otimes\pazocal{O}_{E}
-module which is not of finite type. Hence
I_{m}
has a maximal element, which we denote by
k_{m}
. Because
\tilde{L}_{m+1}+\ell^{m+1}M\subset\tilde{L}_{m}+\ell^{m}M
, we have
k_{m+1}\geq k_{m}
. This shows that
(k_{m})_{m\geq 1}
increases. We show that it is unbounded. Let
n\geq 1
. By Lemma
4.46
, there exists
m\geq n
such that
M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{n}M=\tilde{L}_{m}+\ell^{n}M
. Thus
\tilde{L}_{m}+\ell^{m}M\subset M_{\pazocal{O}}+\ell^{n}M
. In particular, there exists
m\geq n
such that
k_{m}\geq n
.
∎

Proposition 4.48
.

Assume that
\underline{M}
is effective, and let
m\in M
. The following are equivalent:

(i)

m
belongs to the
\ell
-adic closure of
M_{\pazocal{O}}+(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M)
in
M
,

(ii)

for all
n\geq 1
,
m\in\tilde{L}_{n}+(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M)+\ell^{n}M
.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.41
, the inclusion

M_{\pazocal{O}}+(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M)\subset\bigcap_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[\tilde{L}_{n}+(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M)+\ell^{n}M\right]

holds as subsets of
M
, and the right-hand side is
\ell
-adically complete. Hence

(
i
)

implies

(

⁢
i
i

)

. The converse follows from Lemma
4.47
:

\bigcap_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[\tilde{L}_{n}+(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M)+\ell^{n}M\right]\subset\bigcap_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[M_{\pazocal{O}}+(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M)+\ell^{k_{n}}M\right]

where the right-hand side is identified with the
\ell
-adic completion of
M_{\pazocal{O}}+(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M)
.
∎

Néron-Ogg-Shafarevič-type criterion

Let
E
be a local function field with finite residue field
k
and valuation ring
\pazocal{O}_{E}
with maximal ideal
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}_{E}
. This paragraph is an
aparté
offering a good reduction criterion, very much in the spirit of Gardeyn’s Néron-Ogg-Shafarevič-type criterion
[
Gar1
, Thm. 1.1]
. Proposition
4.49
below is not needed in the sequel, although it is useful in examples to compute maximal models.

Consider a characteristic morphism
\kappa:A\to\pazocal{O}_{E}
(
i.e.
an
\mathbb{F}
-algebra morphism) such that
\kappa^{-1}(\mathfrak{p}_{E})\neq(0)
.

Proposition 4.49
.

Let
\underline{M}
be an
A
-motive over
E
, and let
\ell
be a maximal ideal of
A
such that
\kappa(\ell)\pazocal{O}_{E}=\pazocal{O}_{E}
. The following statements are equivalent:

(i)

There exists an
A
-motive
\underline{N}
over
\pazocal{O}_{E}
such that
\underline{N}_{E}
is isomorphic to
\underline{M}
.

(ii)

The inclusion
M_{\operatorname{good}}\subset M_{\pazocal{O}}
is an equality.

(iii)

The representation
\operatorname{T}_{\ell}\underline{M}
is unramified.

Proof.

The equivalence between

(
i
)

and

(

⁢
i
i

)

follows from Theorem
4.41
and Proposition
4.45
. Let
\underline{M}_{n}
denote the Frobenius space
(M/\ell^{n}M,\tau_{M})
of the previous section, and let
L_{n}
and
T_{n}
be its maximal integral and good sublattice respectively. The equivalence between

(

⁢
i
i

)

and

(

⁢
i
i
i

)

follows from the following sequence of equivalent statements:

\displaystyle\operatorname{T}_{\ell}\underline{M}~\text{is~unramified}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\forall n\geq 1,~T\underline{M}_{n}~\text{is~unramified}\quad(\text{notations~of~Thm.}~\ref{thm:katz-equivalence})

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\forall n\geq 1,~T_{n}=L_{n}\quad(\text{by~Prop.}~\ref{prop:babyNOS})

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow M_{\text{good}}=M_{\pazocal{O}}\quad(\text{by~Thm.~\ref{thm:integral-model:motive-to-frobenius-module}~and~Prop.~\ref{prop:From-good-models-to-Frob-spaces}}).

∎

Definition 4.50
.

We say that
\underline{M}
has
good reduction
if one of the equivalent points of Proposition
4.49
is satisfied.

4.3
Models of
A
-motives over a global function field

Let
R
be a Dedekind domain which is an
A
-algebra via a morphism
\kappa
. We assume that the only ideal mapped to the zero ideal under the induced map
\kappa^{*}:\operatorname{Spec}R\to\operatorname{Spec}A
is the zero ideal itself; equivalently, for all prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}\subset R
, we have
\kappa^{-1}(\mathfrak{p})=(0)
if and only if
\mathfrak{p}=(0)
. In particular, the results of Subsection
4.2
holds for
\pazocal{O}_{E}
being either the localization
R_{{(\mathfrak{p})}}
of
R
at the multiplicative subset
A\setminus\mathfrak{p}
of
A
or its completion
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Let
F
be the fraction field of
R
, and
F_{\mathfrak{p}}
the fraction field of
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

In this subsection, we show existence of maximal
R
-model for
A
-motives over
F
and compare them with maximal local models of Subsection
4.2
. Let
\underline{M}=(M,\tau_{M})
be an
A
-motive over
F
, and let
\underline{M}_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}
be the
A
-motive over
F_{\mathfrak{p}}
obtained from
\underline{M}
by base change from
F
to
F_{\mathfrak{p}}
. We let
M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
denote the maximal
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
-model of
\underline{M}_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}
whose existence is ensured by Proposition
4.30
.

Proposition 4.51
.

There exists a unique maximal
R
-model for
\underline{M}
. It equals the intersection
\bigcap_{\mathfrak{p}}(M\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}})
for
\mathfrak{p}
running over the maximal ideals of
R
. We denote it by
M_{R}
.

Proof.

From Lemma
4.21
, uniqueness is automatic. Let
N
be an
R
-model for
\underline{M}
(whose existence is ensured by Proposition
4.20
). For any maximal ideal
\mathfrak{p}
of
R
, we have
N\subset N\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}\subset M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
by maximality of
M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
. Therefore
N\subset\bigcap_{\mathfrak{p}}(M\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}})
. Hence, it is sufficient to show that
\bigcap_{\mathfrak{p}}(M\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}})
is an
R
-model. First note that it is a sub-
A\otimes R
-module of
M
which, as it contains
N
, generates
M
over
F
. To show stability by
\tau_{M}
, let
e
be a large enough integer such that
\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}M)\subset\mathfrak{j}^{-e}M
. One easily checks that:

\tau_{M}\left(\tau^{*}\bigcap_{\mathfrak{p}}(M\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}})\right)\subset\bigcap_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathfrak{j}^{-e}(M\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}})\subset\left(\bigcap_{\mathfrak{p}}M\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}\right)[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}].

It remains to show that
\bigcap_{\mathfrak{p}}(M\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}})
is finitely generated over
A\otimes R
. Let
T\subset M
be a generating finite flat sub-
A\otimes R
-module and let
d\in R
be such that
d\cdot T\subset\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}T)[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
. In particular,
T_{\mathfrak{p}}:=T\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}
is a generating finite flat sub-
A\otimes R_{\mathfrak{p}}
-module of
M_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and
[T_{\mathfrak{p}}:\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}T_{\mathfrak{p}})]\leq v_{\mathfrak{p}}(d)
. We denote
{c_{T}(\mathfrak{p}):=\lfloor-[T_{\mathfrak{p}}:\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}T_{\mathfrak{p}})]/(q-1)\rfloor}
so that, as in the proof of Proposition
4.30
,
M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}\subset\varpi^{c_{T}(\mathfrak{p})}T_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Note that
c_{T}(\mathfrak{p})=0
for almost all
\mathfrak{p}
. We have

M\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}\subset M\cap\varpi^{c_{T}(\mathfrak{p})}T_{\mathfrak{p}}=(F\otimes_{R}T)\cap(\mathfrak{p}^{c_{T}(\mathfrak{p})}R_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes_{R}T)=\mathfrak{p}^{c_{T}(\mathfrak{p})}T

where we used the flatness of
T
for the last equality. Therefore,

\bigcap_{\mathfrak{p}}(M\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}})\subset\bigcap_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathfrak{p}^{c_{T}(\mathfrak{p})}T=\mathfrak{d}\cdot T,

where
\mathfrak{d}:=\cap_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathfrak{p}^{c_{T}(\mathfrak{p})}
is a fractional ideal of
R
. The module
\mathfrak{d}\cdot T
being finitely generated, we conclude using the Noetherianity of
A\otimes R
.
∎

From Lemma
4.23
, we obtain:

Corollary 4.52
.

The maximal good sublattice
M_{\operatorname{good}}
of
\underline{M}
exists and is unique.

We now state the global version of Theorem
4.32
. The argument is similar, so we omit proofs.

Theorem 4.53
.

Both
M_{R}
and
M_{\operatorname{good}}
are projective over
A\otimes R
.

Remark 4.54
.

Note, however, that an integral model for
\underline{M}
, when not maximal, is not necessarily projective. For instance, the
\mathbb{F}[t]
-motive
\mathbbm{1}=(\mathbb{F}[t](\theta),\mathbf{1})
over
\mathbb{F}(\theta)
admits
L:=t\mathbb{F}[t,\theta]+\theta\mathbb{F}[t,\theta]
as
\mathbb{F}[\theta]
-model. But it is well-known that
L
is not a flat
\mathbb{F}[t,\theta]
-module. A short way to see this consists in considering the element
\Delta:=(t\otimes\theta-\theta\otimes t)\in L\otimes_{\mathbb{F}[t,\theta]}L
.
\Delta
is nonzero in
L\otimes_{\mathbb{F}[t,\theta]}L
, but

\theta\cdot\Delta=(\theta t)\otimes\theta-\theta\otimes(\theta t)=(\theta t)\otimes\theta-(\theta t)\otimes\theta=0.

Then
L
is not flat because
L\otimes_{\mathbb{F}[t,\theta]}L
has non trivial torsion.

By the formula given in Proposition
4.51
, the datum of all the maximal local models is enough to recover
M_{R}
. Conversely, the knowledge of
M_{R}
is sufficient to recover
M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
as we show next:

Theorem 4.55
.

The inclusion
M_{R}\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}\subseteq M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is an equality.

We begin with a useful statement on its own which allows to reduce the proof of Theorem
4.55
to the case where
R
is a discrete valuation ring. We denote by
(-)_{(\mathfrak{p})}
the localization functor with respect to the multiplicative subset
A\setminus\mathfrak{p}
of
A
. We denote by
M_{R_{(\mathfrak{p})}}
the maximal
R_{(\mathfrak{p})}
-model of
\underline{M}
.

Lemma 4.56
.

The inclusion
(M_{R})_{(\mathfrak{p})}\subseteq M_{R_{(\mathfrak{p})}}
is an equality.

Proof.

As both modules generate
M
over
F
, the cokernel of the inclusion is
\mathfrak{p}
-torsion and thus, given any
m\in M_{R_{(\mathfrak{p})}}
, there exists
a\in A
such that
(a)=\mathfrak{p}^{c}
for some
c\geq 1
and
am\in(M_{R})_{(\mathfrak{p})}
. By definition of localization, there exists
b\in A\setminus\mathfrak{p}
for which
abm\in M_{R}
. By Proposition
4.51
, we have
abm\in M\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{q}}}
and then
bm\in M\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{q}}}
for all
\mathfrak{q}\neq\mathfrak{p}
. Hence,

bm\in M_{R_{(\mathfrak{p})}}\bigcap_{\mathfrak{q}\neq\mathfrak{p}}(M\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{q}}})=(M\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}})\bigcap_{\mathfrak{q}\neq\mathfrak{p}}(M\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{q}}})=M_{R},

and finally
m\in(M_{R})_{(\mathfrak{p})}
. For the first equality, we used that
M_{R_{(\mathfrak{p})}}=M\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
, which follows by applying Proposition
4.51
but to the ring
R_{(\mathfrak{p})}
instead of
R
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.55
.

In virtue of Lemma
4.56
, we may assume that
R
is a discrete valuation ring with maximal ideal
\mathfrak{p}
and that
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
is its completion. Let
C
denote the cokernel of
M_{R}\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}\subseteq M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
; as
C
is both finite as a module over
A\otimes R_{\mathfrak{p}}
and is
\operatorname{id}\otimes\mathfrak{p}
-torsion, it is finite over
A
.

Let
\ell
be a maximal ideal of
A
not lying under
\mathfrak{p}
(
i.e.
such that condition

(

C
ℓ

)

holds). Let
(k_{n})_{n}
and
(k_{n}^{\prime})_{n}
be the sequences of integers produced by Lemma
4.47
for
\underline{M}
and
\underline{M}_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}
respectively. Let
n
be such that
k_{n},k_{n}^{\prime}\geq 1
. Let
L
and
L_{\mathfrak{p}}
be the maximal
R
-model and
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
-models of the Frobenius spaces
\underline{M}/\ell^{n}\underline{M}
and
\underline{M}_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}/\ell^{n}\underline{M}_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}
. As remarked in
4.5
, we have
L_{\mathfrak{p}}=L\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}
. By choice of
n
, we also have

\displaystyle L+\ell M=M_{R}+\ell M

(4.8)

\displaystyle L_{\mathfrak{p}}+\ell M_{\mathfrak{p}}=M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}+\ell M_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}

(4.9)

Using
F_{\mathfrak{p}}=F\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}
, we get:

\displaystyle M_{R}\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}+\ell M_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}

\displaystyle=(M_{R}+\ell M)\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}=(L+\ell M)\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}=L\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}+\ell M_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}=L_{\mathfrak{p}}+\ell M_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}

\displaystyle=M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}+\ell M_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}.

As a result, we obtain:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(M_{R}\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}+\ell M_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}})/\ell M_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}+\ell M_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}})/\ell M_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(M_{R}\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}})/\ell(M_{R}\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}/\ell M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}

That the vertical arrows are isomorphism as depicted on the diagram is deduced from the flatness of the modules
M_{R}\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
over
A\otimes R_{\mathfrak{p}}
(
e.g.
as in the beginning of the proof of Lemma
4.28
). This implies that the bottom row is an isomorphism, and hence,
C/\ell C=(0)
.

This being true for infinitely many
\ell
, and because
C
is a finite
A
-module, we obtain
C=(0)
, proving the theorem.
∎

Definition 4.57
.

We say that
\underline{M}
has
good reduction at
\mathfrak{p}
if
\underline{M}_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}
has good reduction. We say that
\underline{M}
has
everywhere good reduction
if
\underline{M}
has good reduction at
\mathfrak{p}
for all maximal ideals
\mathfrak{p}
of
R
.

4.4
The integral part of
A
-motivic cohomology

In this subsection, we introduce the notion of
integral
and
\mathfrak{p}
-integral
extension and compare it with that of
good extension at
\mathfrak{p}
with respect to
\ell
as defined earlier in Definition
2.30
. More precisely, we shall prove Theorems C and D stated in the introduction.

Over local function fields

Let
\mathfrak{m}\subset A
be a maximal ideal of
A
with associated local function field
K_{\mathfrak{m}}
. Let
F_{\mathfrak{p}}
be a finite field extension of
K_{\mathfrak{m}}
with valuation ring
\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Let
\kappa:A\to\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}
be the inclusion; in particular,
\kappa^{-1}(\mathfrak{p})\neq(0)
and the results of the previous subsections apply. Let
F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\operatorname{ur}}
be the maximal unramified extension of
F_{\mathfrak{p}}
in
F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{s}
. Let
I_{\mathfrak{p}}
be the inertia subgroup of
G_{\mathfrak{p}}=G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.

Let
\underline{M}
be an
A
-motive over
F_{\mathfrak{p}}
and let
M_{\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}}
be its maximal
\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}
-model.

Definition 4.58
.

We define
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})
as the sub-
A
-module of
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathscr{M}_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})
given by the image of
M_{\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
through
\iota
(Theorem
2.21
). Extensions whose class belongs to the latter module will be called
\mathfrak{p}
-integral
(or simply
integral
when
\mathfrak{p}
is clear from the context).

From Corollary
4.25
,
\iota
induces an isomorphism of
A
-modules:

\frac{M_{\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]}{(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M_{\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M}).

Remark 4.59
.

An important remark is that the assignment
\underline{M}\mapsto\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})
is functorial, thanks to Corollary
4.26
.

Our main result states that for
\ell\neq\mathfrak{m}
integral extensions have good reduction with respect to
\ell
:

Theorem 4.60
.

Let
\ell
be a maximal ideal in
A
distinct from
\mathfrak{m}
. Then,

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})\subset\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{good}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})_{\ell}.

Proof.

By choice of
\ell
, we have
\kappa(\ell)\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}=\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Let
[\underline{E}]\in\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})
. By definition, there exists
m\in M_{\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
such that
[\underline{E}]=\iota(m)
. If
\tilde{L}_{n}
denotes a lift in
M
of the maximal integral model of the Frobenius space
\underline{M}/\ell^{n}\underline{M}
, we obtain
m\in\tilde{L}_{n}+\ell^{n}M
for all
n
. By Proposition
4.18
, there exists
y_{n}\in M\otimes_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}\otimes F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\text{nr}}
such that

m\equiv y_{n}-\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}y_{n})\pmod{\ell^{n}}.

(4.10)

Note that, for each
n
, there are only finitely many such
y_{n}\pmod{\ell^{n}}
. We next show that we can choose
compatibly

y_{n}
for all
n
(that is
y_{n+1}\equiv y_{n}\pmod{\ell^{n}}
). Let us define a tree
T
indexed by
n\geq 1
whose nodes at the height
n
are the solutions
y_{n}
of (
4.10
) in
(M\otimes_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\operatorname{ur}})/\ell^{n}(M\otimes_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\operatorname{ur}})
. There is an edge between
z_{n}
and
z_{n+1}
if and only if
z_{n+1}
coincides with
z_{n}
modulo
\ell^{n}
. The tree has finitely many nodes at each height and it is infinite from the fact that a solution of (
4.10
) exists for all
n
. By König’s Lemma, there exists an infinite branch on
T
. This branch corresponds to a converging sequence
(y_{n})_{n\geq 1}
whose limit
y
in
(M\hat{\otimes}_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\operatorname{ur}})_{\ell}
satisfies
m=y-\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}y)
. Therefore, we conclude that
[\underline{E}]\in\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\text{good}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})_{\ell}
thanks to Proposition
2.31
.
∎

Over global function fields

Let
F
be a finite field extension of
K
and let
\pazocal{O}_{F}
be the integral closure of
A
in
F
. We let
\kappa:A\to\pazocal{O}_{F}
denote the inclusion. We fix
S
to be a set of nonzero prime ideals of
\pazocal{O}_{F}
and consider the subring
R:=\pazocal{O}_{F}[S^{-1}]
of
F
. The ring
R
is a Dedekind domain whose fraction field is
F
. We have
\kappa^{-1}(\mathfrak{p})=(0)
if and only if
\mathfrak{p}=(0)
, so the result of Subsection
4.3
apply.

Let
\underline{M}=(M,\tau_{M})
be an Anderson
A
-motive over
F
. Given a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{p}\subset R
, we let
\underline{M}_{\mathfrak{p}}
be the
A
-motive over
F_{\mathfrak{p}}
obtained from
\underline{M}
by base-change from
F
to
F_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Given an extension
[\underline{E}]\in\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathscr{M}_{F}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})
, the exactness of the base change functor defines an extension
[\underline{E}_{\mathfrak{p}}]\in\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathscr{M}_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{1}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{p}},\underline{M}_{\mathfrak{p}})
. This allows us to define the following submodule of
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathscr{M}_{F}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})
:

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{R}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})=\bigcap_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\subset R\\
\text{maximal}\end{subarray}}\left\{[\underline{E}]\in\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathscr{M}_{F}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})~\bigg|~[\underline{E}_{\mathfrak{p}}]\in\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{p}},\underline{M}_{\mathfrak{p}})\right\}.

Definition 4.61
.

We say that an extension of
\mathbbm{1}
by
\underline{M}
is
R
-integral
(or simply
integral
) if it belongs to
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{R}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})
.

Our second main result consists of the next theorem.

Theorem 4.62
.

Let
M_{R}
denote the maximal integral
R
-model of
\underline{M}
. The
A
-module
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{R}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})
equals the image of
M_{R}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
through
\iota
. In addition,
\iota
induces a natural isomorphism of
A
-modules:

\frac{M_{R}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]}{(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M_{R})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{R}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M}).

The proof of the above theorem will result after a sequence of lemmas.

Lemma 4.63
.

Let
M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
be the maximal integral model of
\underline{M}_{\mathfrak{p}}=(M_{\mathfrak{p}},\tau_{M})
. Inside
M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
, we have:

M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]\cap\left(M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]+(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M_{\mathfrak{p}})\right)=M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]+(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M).

Proof.

The inclusion
\supset
is clear. Since
M
is generated over
F
by elements in
M\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
and as
F_{\mathfrak{p}}=F+R_{\mathfrak{p}}
, we have
M_{\mathfrak{p}}=M+M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
. Let
m
be an element in the left-hand side. We can write
m
as
m_{\mathfrak{p}}+n_{\mathfrak{p}}-\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}n_{\mathfrak{p}})+n-\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}n)
where
m_{\mathfrak{p}}\in M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
,
n_{\mathfrak{p}}\in M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
and
n\in M
. In particular,
m_{\mathfrak{p}}+n_{\mathfrak{p}}-\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}n_{\mathfrak{p}})
belongs to
M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
which implies that
m\in M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]+(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M)
.
∎

Lemma 4.64
.

Let
m\in M
. Then
m\in M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
for almost all maximal ideals
\mathfrak{p}
of
R
.

Proof.

There exists a nonzero element
d\in R
such that
dm\in M_{R}
. Let
\{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{q}_{s}\}
be the finite set of maximal ideals in
R
that contain
(d)
. By Proposition
4.51
,
m\in M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
for all
\mathfrak{p}
not in
\{\mathfrak{q}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{q}_{s}\}
.
∎

Let
N
be a finite dimensional vector space over
F
(resp.
F_{\mathfrak{p}}
). By a
lattice in
N
we mean a finitely generated module over
R
(resp.
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
) in
N
that contains a basis of
N
.

Lemma 4.65

(Strong approximation)
.

Let
N
be a finite dimensional
F
-vector space and, for all maximal ideals
\mathfrak{p}
of
R
, let
N_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
be an
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
-lattice in
N_{\mathfrak{p}}:=N\otimes_{R}F_{\mathfrak{p}}
such that the intersection
\bigcap_{\mathfrak{p}}\left(N\cap N_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}\right)
, over all maximal ideals
\mathfrak{p}
of
R
, is an
R
-lattice in
N
. Let
T
be a finite set of maximal ideals in
R
and, for
\mathfrak{q}\in T
, let
n_{\mathfrak{q}}\in N_{\mathfrak{q}}
. Then, there exists
n\in N
such that
n-n_{\mathfrak{q}}\in N_{R_{\mathfrak{q}}}
for all
\mathfrak{q}\in T
and
n\in N_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
for all
\mathfrak{p}
not in
T
.

Proof.

Let
N_{R}
denote the intersection
\bigcap_{\mathfrak{p}}\left(N\cap N_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}\right)
over all maximal ideals
\mathfrak{p}
of
R
. By the structure Theorem for finitely generated modules over the Dedekind domain
R
, there exists a nonzero ideal
\mathfrak{a}\subset R
and elements
\{b_{1},\ldots,b_{r}\}\subset M
such that

N_{R}=Rb_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus Rb_{r-1}\oplus\mathfrak{a}b_{r}.

Because
N_{R}\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}\subset N_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
for
\mathfrak{p}\subset R
, we have
R_{\mathfrak{p}}b_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{v_{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathfrak{a})}R_{\mathfrak{p}}b_{r}\subset N_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
. For
\mathfrak{q}\in T
, let us write
n_{\mathfrak{q}}=\sum_{i}{f_{\mathfrak{q},i}b_{i}}
with
f_{\mathfrak{q},i}\in F_{\mathfrak{q}}
. By the strong approximation Theorem
[
Ros
, Thm. 6.13]
, for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,r\}
, there exists
f_{i}\in F
such that

1.

for
\mathfrak{q}\in T
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,r-1\}
,
v_{\mathfrak{q}}(f_{i}-f_{\mathfrak{q},i})\geq 0
,

2.

for
\mathfrak{q}\in T
,
v_{\mathfrak{q}}(f_{r}-f_{\mathfrak{q},r})\geq v_{\mathfrak{q}}(\mathfrak{a})
,

3.

for
\mathfrak{p}\notin T
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,r-1\}
,
v_{\mathfrak{p}}(f_{i})\geq 0
,

4.

for
\mathfrak{p}\notin T
,
v_{\mathfrak{p}}(f_{r})\geq v_{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathfrak{a})
.

The element
n=\sum_{i}{f_{i}b_{i}}\in N
satisfies the assumption of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 4.66
.

We have

\bigcap_{\mathfrak{p}\subset R}\left(M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]+(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M)\right)=M_{R}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]+(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M)

(4.11)

where the intersection is indexed over the maximal ideals of
R
.

Proof.

The inclusion
\supset
follows from Proposition
4.51
. Conversely, let
m
be an element of the left-hand side of (
4.11
). By Lemma
4.64
, there exists a finite subset
T
of maximal ideals of
R
such that
m\in M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
for
\mathfrak{p}\notin T
. For
\mathfrak{q}\in T
, there exists
n_{\mathfrak{q}}\in M
and
m_{\mathfrak{q}}\in M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{q}}}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
such that
m=m_{\mathfrak{q}}+n_{\mathfrak{q}}-\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}n_{\mathfrak{q}})
.

Let
N
be a finite dimensional sub-
F
-vector space of
M
that contains
m
and
n_{\mathfrak{q}}
for all
\mathfrak{q}\in T
. For a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{p}
of
R
, let
N_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}:=M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}\cap(N\otimes_{F}F_{\mathfrak{p}})
. Let also
N_{R}:=\bigcap_{\mathfrak{p}}(N\cap N_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}})
. By Proposition
4.51
, we have
N_{R}=N\cap M_{R}
. In particular, this implies that
N_{R}
generates
N
over
F
. This also implies that
N_{R}
is a finitely generated
R
-module : as
M_{R}
is finite projective (Theorem
4.53
), it is included in a finite free
A\otimes R
-module from which we consider a basis
(m_{1},\ldots,m_{t})
. On the other-hand, if
(n_{1},\ldots,n_{s})
is a basis of
N
over
F
, there exists a constant
C\geq 0
large enough for which each
n_{i}
belongs to
\bigoplus_{j}(A\otimes F)_{\deg<C}\cdot m_{j}
,
i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}
, where we denoted
(A\otimes F)_{\deg<C}
the finite dimensional sub-
F
-vector space of
A\otimes F
of elements built as sums of elementary tensors
a\otimes f
with
\deg(a)<C
. This yields:

N_{R}\subseteq\bigoplus_{j=1}^{t}(A\otimes R)_{\deg<C}\cdot m_{j}

and hence
N_{R}
is finitely generated. We have therefore proved that
N_{R}
is an
R
-lattice in
N
, and Lemma
4.65
applies: there exists
n\in N
such that
n-n_{\mathfrak{q}}\in N_{R_{\mathfrak{q}}}
for all
\mathfrak{q}\in T
and
n\in N_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}
for all
\mathfrak{p}
not in
T
. Then
m+n-\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}n)\in N_{R}\subset M_{R}
, which ends the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.62
.

Let
[\underline{E}]\in\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathscr{M}_{F}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})
and let
m\in M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
be such that
[\underline{E}]=\iota(m)
. The proof of Theorem
4.62
is achieved via the sequence of equivalence:

\displaystyle[\underline{E}]\in\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{R}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\forall\mathfrak{p}\in\operatorname{Spm}R:~[\underline{E}_{\mathfrak{p}}]\in\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{p}},\underline{M}_{\mathfrak{p}})

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\forall\mathfrak{p}\in\operatorname{Spm}R:~m\in M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]\cap[M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]+(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M_{\mathfrak{p}})]

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\forall\mathfrak{p}\in\operatorname{Spm}R:~m\in M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]\cap M_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]+(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow m\in M_{R}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]+(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow[\underline{E}]\in\iota(M_{R}[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}])

where the second equivalence stems from Definition
4.19
, the third from Lemma
4.63
and the fourth from Lemma
4.66
. The second assertion follows from Corollary
4.25
.
∎

5
Regulated extensions

Let
\mathfrak{m}\subset A
be a maximal ideal of
A
with associated local function field
K_{\mathfrak{m}}
, let
F_{\mathfrak{p}}
be a finite field extension of
K_{\mathfrak{m}}
with valuation ring
\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}
and, as before, denote by
\kappa:A\to\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}
the inclusion.

Consider an
A
-motive
\underline{M}
over
\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}
. In the previous section, we proved that given any maximal ideal
\ell
in
A
distinct from
\mathfrak{m}
, there is an inclusion:

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})\subset\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\text{good}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})_{\ell}

(5.1)

of sub-
A
-modules of
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathscr{M}_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})
. Surprisingly, this is almost never an equality. In Subsection
5.1
, we construct explicitly a class in the right-hand side of (
5.1
) which does not belong the left-hand side. In the remaining part of this text, we offer a conjectural framework in which we expect to solve this default.

5.1
A particular extension of
\mathbbm{1}
by itself

We consider the case where
A=\mathbb{F}[t]
and consider the maximal ideal
\ell=(t)
in
A
. Let
E
be the local function field
\mathbb{F}(\!(\pi)\!)
,
\pazocal{O}=\mathbb{F}[\![\pi]\!]
, with structure morphism
\kappa:A\to\pazocal{O}
defined by
\kappa(t)=1+\pi
(that is,
\pi=\theta-1
where
\theta=\kappa(t)
). We have
\kappa(\ell)\pazocal{O}=(1+\pi)\mathbb{F}[\![\pi]\!]=\pazocal{O}
so
\kappa(\ell)\pazocal{O}=\pazocal{O}
. Let
\underline{M}=\mathbbm{1}
over
E
. By Proposition
2.29
enriched with Proposition
4.18
, there is a commutative square of
A
-modules:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathscr{M}_{E}}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}^{1}(I_{E},T_{(t)}\mathbbm{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\frac{E[t,\frac{1}{t-\theta}]}{(\operatorname{id}-\tau)(E[t])}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\frac{E^{\operatorname{ur}}[\![t]\!]}{\pazocal{O}[\![t]\!]+(\operatorname{id}-\tau)(E[\![t]\!])}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}

where the bottom arrow is induced by the inclusion of
E[t,(t-\theta)^{-1}]
in
E[\![t]\!]
.

Hereafter, we construct an element
m
in
E[t,(t-\theta)^{-1}]
of the form

m=\frac{m_{k}}{(t-\theta)^{k}}+\cdots+\frac{m_{1}}{(t-\theta)}

for some
m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}
in
E
, not all in
\pazocal{O}
, such that
m
belongs to
(\operatorname{id}-\tau)(E[\![t]\!])
. Then,
\iota(m)
has good reduction with respect to
(t)
(in the sense of Definition
2.30
) but does not belong to
\pazocal{O}[t,(t-\theta)^{-1}]+(\operatorname{id}-\tau)(E[t])
.

Let
k=q^{2}
where
q
is the number of elements of
\mathbb{F}
, and for
i\in\{0,\ldots,k-1\}
, define
n^{\prime}_{i}
as
\theta^{i}(\pi^{-1}-\pi^{-q})
in
E
. So
n_{i}^{\prime}
has valuation
-q
. For all
c\geq 0
, let
f_{ck}
be a root in
E
of the polynomial:

X^{q}-X+\theta^{-ck}n^{\prime}_{0}.

Such a root exists in
E
as
\theta^{-k}\equiv 1\pmod{\pi^{q^{2}}}
. We now define
f_{l}\in E
for all
l\geq 0
by the rule
f_{l}:=f_{ck}
if
l=ck+r
for
c\geq 0
and
0\leq r<k
. We obtain

\text{for~all~}l\geq 0:\quad\theta^{-l}n^{\prime}_{\bar{l}}=f_{l}-f_{l}^{q}

(5.2)

where
\overline{l}\in\{0,\ldots,k-1\}
denotes the remainder of the euclidean division of
l
by
k
.

For
l\geq 0
, let
S_{k}(l)
be the
Pascal matrix
whose
i
th row-
j
th column entry is the binomial coefficient
\binom{i+j+l}{i+l}
(
0\leq i,j<k
). The following claims are easily proven:

(i)

The determinant of
S_{k}(0)
is
1
.

(ii)

Let
p
be the characteristic of
\mathbb{F}
. For
l\geq 0
, we have the formula

S_{k}(l+1)\equiv\begin{pmatrix}&1&&&\\
&&1&&\\
&&&\ddots&\\
&&&&1\\
1&&&&\end{pmatrix}S_{k}(l)\pmod{p}

(iii)

The application
l\mapsto S_{k}(l)
is
k
-periodic modulo
p
.

We now define
m_{i}^{\prime}\in E
for
i\in\{0,\ldots,k-1\}
by mean of the formula:

S_{k}(0)\begin{pmatrix}m_{0}^{\prime}\\
m_{1}^{\prime}\\
\vdots\\
m_{k-1}^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}n_{0}^{\prime}\\
n_{1}^{\prime}\\
\vdots\\
n_{k-1}^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}

Since the
n_{i}
’s have negative valuation, at least one of the
m_{i}
’s has negative valuation (by

(
i
)

). From

(

⁢
i
i

)

, we have

\text{for~all~}l\geq 0:\quad S_{k}(l)\begin{pmatrix}m_{0}^{\prime}\\
m_{1}^{\prime}\\
\vdots\\
m_{k-1}^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}n_{\bar{l}}^{\prime}\\
n_{\overline{l+1}}^{\prime}\\
\vdots\\
n_{\overline{l+k-1}}^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}

From (
5.2
), we obtain:

\text{for~all~}l\geq 0:\quad\theta^{-l}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}{m^{\prime}_{i}\binom{i+l}{l}}\right)=f_{l}-f_{l}^{q}.

(5.3)

Finally, for
i\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
, let
m_{i}:=(-\theta)^{i}m_{i-1}^{\prime}
. Formula (
5.3
) amounts to:

m:=\frac{m_{k}}{(t-\theta)^{k}}+\ldots+\frac{m_{1}}{(t-\theta)}=f-f^{(1)}

where
f:=\sum_{l\geq 0}{f_{l}t^{l}}
. Therefore
\iota(m)
has good reduction, although
m
does not belong to
\pazocal{O}[t,(t-\theta)^{-1}]+(\operatorname{id}-\tau)(E[t])
.

5.2
Hodge poylgons of
A
-motives

We recognize that the extension of
Hodge-Pink structures
corresponding to
\iota(m)
constructed in the previous subsection is not
Hodge additive
in the sense of
[
Pin
, §6 & 7]
. We now introduce the notion of
regulated extensions
which is the counterpart of Hodge additivity for
A
-motives.

Let
F
be (any) field equipped with an
\mathbb{F}
-algebra morphism
\kappa:A\to F
. Let
\underline{M}
be an
A
-motive of rank
r
over
F
and characteristic morphism
\kappa
. Given
e
a large enough integer for which
\mathfrak{j}^{e}\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}M)\subset M
, we have an isomorphism

M/\mathfrak{j}^{e}\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}M)\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}(A\otimes F)/\mathfrak{j}^{e+w_{i}}

for some uniquely determined integers
w_{1}\leq\cdots\leq w_{r}
independent of
e
nor of the isomorphism. We call
(w_{1},\ldots,w_{r})
the
multiset of Hodge weights of
\underline{M}
. Hodge weights are graphically organized into the
Hodge polygon of
\underline{M}
whose construction we now recall.

By
polygon
, we mean the graph in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
of a piecewise linear convex function
[0,n]\to\mathbb{R}
starting at
(0,0)
. All slopes are assumed to be rational numbers, and the length of the subinterval of
[0,n]
on which the function as a given slope
q\in\mathbb{Q}
is called the
multiplicity of
q
. Therefore, each polygon
P
is uniquely determined by its
multiplicity function

m_{P}:\mathbb{Q}\to\mathbb{N}
which assigns to
q\in\mathbb{Q}
the multiplicity of the slope
q
. Such a function
m
is the multiplicity function of a polygon if, and only if, the set
\{q\in\mathbb{Q}|m(q)>0\}
is finite. The
Hodge Polygon of
\underline{M}
is the polygon whose multiplicity function is
q\mapsto\#\{i\in\{1,\ldots,r\}|w_{i}=q\}
.

Let
0\to\underline{M}\to\underline{E}\to\underline{N}\to 0
be an exact sequence of
A
-motives over
F
. An argument due to Katz shows that the Hodge Polygon of
\underline{M}\oplus\underline{N}
lies above that of
\underline{E}
and has the same end points (see
[
Kat3
, Lem. 1.2.3]
in the context of
F
-crystals, or
[
Pin
, Prop. 6.9]
in our situation). In general, one cannot claim equality among those Hodge Polygons which makes our situation differ from classical mixed motives over
\mathbb{Q}
. In the context of function fields Hodge structures, in order the compute Hodge groups explicitly, Pink introduced the condition of
Hodge additivity

[
Pin
, §6 & 7]
whose counterpart for
A
-motives is that of
regulation
.

Definition 5.1
.

We call an exact sequence
0\to\underline{M}\to\underline{E}\to\underline{N}\to 0
in
\mathscr{M}_{F}

regulated
if the Hodge polygon of
\underline{M}\oplus\underline{N}
coincide with that of
\underline{E}
.

Clearly, any exact sequence equivalent to a regulated exact sequence in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathscr{M}_{F}}(\underline{N},\underline{M})
is itself regulated. We denote by
\operatorname{Ext}^{1,\operatorname{reg}}_{\mathscr{M}_{F}}(\underline{N},\underline{M})
the subset of regulated extensions.

Remark 5.2
.

The choice of the naming
regulated
deserves some explanations. It is derived from the name
regulator
which corresponds classically to the group morphism from extensions of classical mixed motives to extensions of classical mixed Hodge structures induced by the exactness of the Hodge realization functor. In function field arithmetic, one associates to an
A
-motive
\underline{M}
the data of an
F
-vector space equipped with a decreasing filtration:

H:=\tau^{*}M/\mathfrak{j}\tau^{*}M,\quad p\in\mathbb{Z}:~\operatorname{Fil}^{p}H:=\operatorname{image}\left(\tau^{*}M\cap\mathfrak{j}^{p}\cdot\tau_{M}^{-1}(M)\to\tau^{*}M/\mathfrak{j}\tau^{*}M\right),

(5.4)

which one could legitimately called
its associated Hodge structure

(H,\operatorname{Fil})
(
e.g.

[
HaJu
]
). However, following considerations by Pink, this functor is not exact
7
7

7

Call a sequence
S
of Hodge structures
exact
if the sequence
\operatorname{Fil}^{p}S
is exact in the category of
F
-vector spaces for all
p
.
hence preventing the well-definedness of a regulator morphism in this setting. More precisely, as one could derived from
[
Pin
, Prop. 6.11]
, an exact sequence of
A
-motives
\underline{S}
induces an exact sequence of Hodge structures if and only if
\underline{S}
is regulated. The notion of regulation is exploited in a sequel to this text to define a function field regulator among finite dimensional vector spaces (see
[
Gaz
]
and
[
GaMa
]
).

The next proposition allows to compute regulated extensions in the category
\mathscr{M}_{F}
. Let
\underline{M}
and
\underline{N}
be two objects in
\mathscr{M}_{F}
. Recall the map
\iota=\iota_{\underline{N},\underline{M}}
from Proposition
2.16
.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
u\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A\otimes F}(\tau^{*}N,M)[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
and let
[\underline{E}]
be the extension of
\underline{N}
by
\underline{M}
given by
\iota_{\underline{N},\underline{M}}(u)
. Then,
[\underline{E}]
is regulated if and only if there exists
f\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A\otimes F}(\tau^{*}N,\tau^{*}M)
and
g\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A\otimes F}(N,M)
such that
u=\tau_{M}\circ f-g\circ\tau_{N}
.

Proof.

We may assume that
\underline{E}
is the
A
-motive
\left[M\oplus N,\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\tau_{M}&u\\
0&\tau_{N}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right]
(in full generality, the extension
[\underline{E}]
is only equivalent to it). We preserve the notation
E
instead of
M\oplus N
below to lighten notations. For
e\geq 0
large enough, there is an exact sequence of
A\otimes F
-modules

S:\quad 0\longrightarrow M/\mathfrak{j}^{e}\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle m}}{{\longrightarrow}}E/\mathfrak{j}^{e}\tau_{E}(\tau^{*}E)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle n}}{{\longrightarrow}}N/\mathfrak{j}^{e}\tau_{N}(\tau^{*}N)\longrightarrow 0

resulting from the Snake Lemma. We interpret the terms of
S
as finite torsion modules over the discrete valuation ring
F[\![\mathfrak{j}]\!]:=\varprojlim_{n}A\otimes F/\mathfrak{j}^{n}
. The elementary divisors are of the form
e+i
, where
i
runs through the slopes of the corresponding Hodge polygon. Thus, similar to the proof of
[
Pin
, Prop. 8.7]
, the regulation of
[\underline{E}]
is equivalent to the splitting of
S
over
F[\![\mathfrak{j}]\!]
, itself equivalent to the splitting of
S
over
A\otimes F
.

Assume that
[\underline{E}]
is regulated. From a splitting of
S
, we get an isomorphism

\varepsilon:M/\mathfrak{j}^{e}\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}M)\oplus N/\mathfrak{j}^{e}\tau_{N}(\tau^{*}N)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}E/\mathfrak{j}^{e}\tau_{E}(\tau^{*}E)

(5.5)

compatible with
S
. Consider the linear map
\bar{g}:N\to E/\mathfrak{j}^{e}\tau_{E}(\tau^{*}E)
,
n\mapsto\varepsilon(0,n)-(0,n)
, where the subtracted term is understood through the surjection
M\oplus N\twoheadrightarrow E/\mathfrak{j}^{e}\tau_{E}(\tau^{*}E)
. By compatibility of
\varepsilon
with
S
, we have
n\circ\bar{g}=0
, hence
\bar{g}
factors through
m
. As
M
is a projective module,
\bar{g}:N\to M/\mathfrak{j}^{e}\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}M)
lifts to a map
g:N\to M
. By construction, we obtain a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M/\mathfrak{j}^{e}\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}M)\oplus N/\mathfrak{j}^{e}\tau_{N}(\tau^{*}N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E/\mathfrak{j}^{e}\tau_{E}(\tau^{*}E)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M\oplus N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M\oplus N}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{id}_{M}&g\\
0&\operatorname{id}_{N}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}

The bottom row maps
\mathfrak{j}^{e}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\tau_{M}&0\\
0&\tau_{N}\end{smallmatrix}\right)(\tau^{*}M\oplus\tau^{*}N)
isomorphically to
\mathfrak{j}^{e}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\tau_{M}&u\\
0&\tau_{N}\end{smallmatrix}\right)(\tau^{*}M\oplus\tau^{*}N)
. In particular, for any
n\in\tau^{*}N
, there exists a necessarily unique
m^{\prime}\in\tau^{*}M
such that

\begin{pmatrix}\operatorname{id}_{M}&g\\
0&\operatorname{id}_{N}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tau_{M}&0\\
0&\tau_{N}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
n\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\tau_{M}&u\\
0&\tau_{N}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}m^{\prime}\\
n\end{pmatrix}.

The assignment
n\mapsto m^{\prime}
is linear, and we denote it
f
. We have
u=\tau_{M}\circ f-g\circ\tau_{N}
by construction, as desired.

It becomes clear from the proof how to construct a splitting of
S
, from the existence of
f
and
g
such that
u=\tau_{M}\circ f-g\circ\tau_{N}
, proving thusly the converse statement.
∎

It follows from the above proposition that the subset
\operatorname{Ext}^{1,\text{reg}}_{\mathscr{M}_{F}}(\underline{N},\underline{M})
of regulated extensions of
\underline{N}
by
\underline{M}
is well-defined and is a sub-
A
-module of
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathscr{M}_{F}}(\underline{N},\underline{M})
. We also deduce that the notion of regulation is compatible with the duality property of extension modules:

Corollary 5.4
.

Let
d:\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathscr{M}_{F}}(\underline{N},\underline{M})\to\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathscr{M}_{F}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M}\otimes\underline{N}^{\vee})
be the canonical map of (
2.2
). Let
[\underline{E}]
be an extension of
\underline{N}
by
\underline{M}
. Then,
[\underline{E}]
is regulated if and only if
d([\underline{E}])
is.

Proof.

Fix an extension
[\underline{E}]:=[0\to\underline{M}\to\underline{E}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}\underline{N}\to 0]
. Under
d
, it is mapped to the extension
[\underline{E}^{\prime}]:=[0\to\underline{M}\otimes\underline{N}^{\vee}\to\underline{E}^{\prime}\to\mathbbm{1}\to 0]
where
\underline{E}^{\prime}
is the
A
-motive obtained as the pullback of

\underline{E}\otimes\underline{N}^{\vee}\xrightarrow{p\otimes\operatorname{id}}\underline{N}\otimes\underline{N}^{\vee}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varepsilon}}{{\longleftarrow}}\mathbbm{1}

and where
\varepsilon
is the unit morphism (
cf
. Subsection
A.7
of the appendix). Let
u:(\tau^{*}N)[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]\to M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
be an
A\otimes F
-linear map for which
\iota_{\underline{N},\underline{M}}(u)=[\underline{E}]
. This means that, up to replacing
[\underline{E}]
by an equivalent extension, we can assume
\underline{E}
to be the
A
-motive

(E,\tau_{E}):=\left(M\oplus N,\begin{pmatrix}\tau_{M}&u\\
0&\tau_{N}\end{pmatrix}\right)

with
p:E\to N
being the projection
\operatorname{pr}_{N}
. Then,
\underline{E}^{\prime}
is described as the
A
-motive whose underlying module is

E^{\prime}=\left\{v\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A\otimes F}(N,N\oplus M)~|~\operatorname{pr}_{N}\circ g\in(A\otimes F)\cdot\operatorname{id}_{N}\right\}

and where
\tau_{E^{\prime}}
maps
v\in\tau^{*}\operatorname{Hom}(N,E)=\operatorname{Hom}(\tau^{*}N,\tau^{*}E)
to
\tau_{E}\circ v\circ\tau_{N}^{-1}\in\operatorname{Hom}(N,E)[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]
. Now observe that any element in
E^{\prime}
can be written uniquely as
h\oplus a\cdot\operatorname{id}_{N}
for some
h:N\to M
and
a\in A\otimes F
. Taking
v\in E^{\prime}
in this form, we get

\tau_{E^{\prime}}(\tau^{*}v)=(\tau_{M}\circ\tau^{*}v\circ\tau_{N}^{-1}+a\cdot u\circ\tau_{N}^{-1})\oplus a\cdot\operatorname{id}_{N}.

From this computation we deduce that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{A\otimes F}(\tau^{*}N,M)[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathscr{M}_{F}}(\underline{N},\underline{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{A\otimes F}(\tau^{*}(A\otimes F),M\otimes N^{\vee})[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathscr{M}_{F}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M}\otimes\underline{N}^{\vee})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\underline{N},\underline{M}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathbbm{1},\underline{M}\otimes\underline{N}^{\vee}}}

where the left vertical map sends
u
to the morphism mapping
a\in\tau^{*}(A\otimes F)=A\otimes F
to
a\cdot(u\circ\tau_{N}^{-1})
. Applying Proposition
5.3
we get that
[\underline{E}]
is regulated, if and only if there exists
f
and
g
such that
u=g\circ\tau_{N}-\tau_{M}\circ f
, if and only if
u\circ\tau_{N}^{-1}=g-\tau_{M}\circ f\circ\tau_{N}^{-1}
, which happens if and only if
[\underline{E}^{\prime}]
is itself regulated.
∎

In the particular situation of
\underline{N}=\mathbbm{1}
, Proposition
5.3
yields:

Corollary 5.5
.

Let
\underline{M}
be an
A
-motive over
F
. Then,
\iota
induces an isomorphism of
A
-modules:

\frac{M+\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}M)}{(\operatorname{id}-\tau_{M})(M)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Ext}^{1,\operatorname{reg}}_{\mathscr{M}_{F}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M}).

One may use Corollary
5.5
to show that the extension of
\mathbbm{1}
by itself constructed in Subsection
5.1
is not regulated. Indeed, using the notations of Subsection
5.1
, the aforementioned extension was represented by an element

m=\frac{m_{k}}{(t-\theta)^{k}}+\cdots+\frac{m_{1}}{(t-\theta)}\in M[\mathfrak{j}^{-1}]=E[t]\left[\frac{1}{t-\theta}\right]

where at least one of the
m_{i}
’s is non zero. In particular, it does not belong to
M+\tau_{M}(\tau^{*}M)
which is
E[t]
in this case.

Remark 5.6
.

For Hodge-Pink structures, Pink proved that the dimension of
Hodge additive extension spaces
is finite
[
Pin
, Prop. 8.7]
. It seems at first reasonable to expect a similar result for integral regulated extensions of
A
-motives. However, the condition of
regulation
alone is not sufficient to state a counterpart of conjecture
(C

5

)
for
A
-motives. An other condition, that of
analytic reduction at
\infty
, is required. This is the main subject of our sequel
[
Gaz
]
, where we prove the finite generation of the
A
-module of integral regulated extensions having analytic reduction at
\infty
(see Thm. 4.1 in
loc. cit.
).

5.3
Regulated extensions having good reduction

We now assume that
F
is a finite field extension of
K
, let
\mathfrak{p}
be a finite place of
F
and let
\mathfrak{m}
be the place of
K
sitting under
\mathfrak{p}
. Hereafter,
\kappa
is the inclusion of
A
into
F
; it factors through
\pazocal{O}_{F}
, the integral closure of
A
in
F
. By the field
L
(resp. the ring
\pazocal{O}_{L}
) we shall mean either
F
or
F_{\mathfrak{p}}
(resp.
\pazocal{O}_{F}
or
\pazocal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}
). Let
\ell
be a maximal ideal in
A
distinct from
\mathfrak{m}
.

Let
\underline{M}
be an
A
-motive over
L
. Recall that we considered two submodules of
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathscr{M}_{L}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})
related to integrality and good reduction respectively,

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\pazocal{O}_{L}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})

integral extensions

(Definition
4.58
)

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{good}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})_{\ell}

good reduction extensions w.r.t.
\ell

(Definition
2.30
)

We now introduce their regulated avatar.

Definition 5.7
.

We let
\operatorname{Ext}^{1,\text{reg}}_{\pazocal{O}_{L}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})
be the submodule of
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\pazocal{O}_{L}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})
consisting of regulated extensions. Similarly, by
\operatorname{Ext}^{1,\text{reg}}_{\text{good}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})_{\ell}
we designate the submodule of
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\text{good}}(\mathbbm{1},\underline{M})_{\ell}
consisting of regulated extensions in the category
\mathscr{M}_{L}
.

Assume now that
